[Fremlin’s note](https://www1.essex.ac.uk/maths/people/fremlin/n10805.pdf) · [Pinned Mathlib split source](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Split.lean)

#### Subbox filters and the Cauchy net of tagged sums

**Motivation.** Lemma 2 of the [Henstock–Sacks subsection](#the-henstocksacks-inequality-tagged-sums-over-part-of-a-rectangle) deduces integrability of $F$ on a subbox $J\subseteq K$ from integrability on $K$, and Step 1 of the Henstock–Sacks theorem immediately spends that conclusion to choose a convergence gauge on each cell of $\pi_0$. Two steps in Lemma 2 were deferred there as input (N6d). First, Lemma 1 of that subsection bounds $\lVert S(F,\pi_1)-S(F,\pi_2)\rVert\le\varepsilon$ for admissible prepartitions of $K$ that cover $J$; turning this pairwise estimate into a limit vector $y$ requires completeness of the target space along a non-sequential filter of prepartitions. Second, $y$ is obtained as a limit over prepartitions of the *ambient* box $K$ with union $J$, whereas $\int_J F$ is defined by partitions of $J$ itself. Viewing a partition of $J$ as a prepartition of $K$ leaves its cells, tags and Riemann sum unchanged, yet at the GP parameter it creates a nonempty complement $K\setminus J$ whose chosen partition may require a larger distortion bound. The same filter machinery also supplies the Cauchy criterion for box integrability and the box additivity of $J\mapsto\int_J F$, both of which enter the GP face integrals in the divergence theorem earlier in §3.4.1. This subsection reads those pinned proof bodies, keeps [Cousin's lemma](#cousins-lemma-fine-tagged-partitions-of-a-box), [exact half-open complements](#exact-complements-by-splitting-half-open-boxes) and filter nontriviality as already proved citations, and records the exact one-way role of the remaining $\varepsilon$–gauge clause in (N6a).

**Example.** On $K=(0,2]$ and its subbox $J=(0,1]$, define $F:\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$ by $F(x)=\mathbf 1_{x\le 1/2}+4i\,\mathbf 1_{x=1}$. A tagged partition $\pi$ of $J$ is already a finite family of pairwise disjoint half-open subintervals of $K$ with tags in $\bar J=[0,1]\subseteq[0,2]$, so embedding $\pi$ into $K$ changes neither a single cell nor a single tag, and $S(F,\pi)$ is literally the same complex number in both roles. Two fine Henstock partitions $\pi_1,\pi_2$ of $J$ can assign different lengths to a cell tagged at the spike $x=1$, and any fine Henstock completion $Q$ of $(1,2]$ whose leftmost cell is tagged at $1$ picks up its own imaginary spike error $S(F,Q)\ne 0$; yet adding the *same* $Q$ to both $\pi_1$ and $\pi_2$ cancels $S(F,Q)$ in $S(F,\pi_1)-S(F,\pi_2)$, making the sums over $J$ Cauchy before $\int_J F=1/2$ is known. In two dimensions, take $J=(0,1/2]^2$ inside $K=(0,2]\times(0,1]$ at the GP parameter. A partition of $J$ into squares sits at distortion level $c=1$ on $J$, where its complement in $J$ is empty. Viewed inside $K$, its endpoint-grid complement on $K\setminus J$ contains $(1/2,2]\times(0,1/2]$ of distortion $3$, so this chosen complement gives the sufficient level $c'=\max(1,3)=3$. The seven-square complement in Hand computation 11 preserves level $1$. Hand computations 10 and 11 work out both examples in full.

**Definitions.** Let $n\ge 1$. As throughout the three preceding subsections, a box $I=\prod_{i=1}^n(l_i,u_i]$ ($l_i<u_i$) is half-open; unions $U(\pi)=\bigcup_{B\in\pi}B$, pairwise disjointness, the partition condition $U(\pi)=I$, and complement equations $U(\pi')=I\setminus U(\pi)$ are exact set relations on half-open subsets of $\mathbb R^n$, whereas tag bounds ($p_B\in\bar I$, and $p_B\in\bar B$ when the Henstock flag is set) and $r$-fineness ($\bar B\subseteq\bar B_\infty(p_B,r(p_B))$) are tested on closures $\bar B=\prod_{i=1}^n[l_i(B),u_i(B)]$. In the pinned generality of `Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean` (note that at commit `0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474` the `BoxIntegral` directory has no `Integration/` subfolder, and the filter file is `Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Filter.lean`), let $E$ and $V$ be real normed spaces, let $f:\mathbb R^n\to E$, and let $\mathrm{vol}$ be a box-additive map sending each box $B$ to a continuous $\mathbb R$-linear operator $\mathrm{vol}(B):E\to V$, meaning $\sum_{B\in\pi}\mathrm{vol}(B)=\mathrm{vol}(J)$ for every untagged partition $\pi$ of every box $J$. The *tagged sum* of $f$ over a tagged prepartition $\pi$ of $K$ is
$$S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)=\sum_{B\in\pi}\mathrm{vol}(B)\bigl(f(p_B)\bigr)\in V .$$
When $E=V$ and $\mathrm{vol}(B)(v)=\lvert B\rvert\,v$ is scalar multiplication by Lebesgue volume (`μ.toBoxAdditive.toSMul`), this is the sum $S(f,\pi)=\sum_{B\in\pi}\lvert B\rvert f(p_B)$ written earlier. [Tagged sums](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L81-L92) · [Box-additive volumes](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Additive.lean#L46-L89)

Fix an integration parameter $\ell=(\mathtt{bRiemann},\mathtt{bHenstock},\mathtt{bDistortion})$. A gauge $r:\mathbb R^n\to(0,\infty)$ is *$\ell$-allowed* (`l.RCond r`) if $\mathtt{bRiemann}=\mathrm{true}$ implies that $r$ is constant; for GP, Henstock and McShane, every positive function is $\ell$-allowed. Notice that $\ell$-allowedness depends only on $\ell$ and $r$, never on the box. Recall that a filter $\mathcal F_1$ on a set $X$ is finer than $\mathcal F_2$, written $\mathcal F_1\le\mathcal F_2$ in Mathlib, when every member of $\mathcal F_2$ belongs to $\mathcal F_1$. Consequently a set $S\subseteq X$ belongs to a supremum $\bigsqcup_{c\ge 0}\mathcal F_c$ if and only if $S\in\mathcal F_c$ **for every** $c\ge 0$. On the set $\mathrm{TaggedPrepartition}(K)$, write $B_{K,c}(r)$ for the set of $(c,r)$-admissible tagged prepartitions of $K$ (`l.MemBaseSet K c r`, conditions (A1)–(A4) of the Henstock–Sacks subsection), and for an untagged prepartition $\pi_0$ of $K$ write $B_{K,c,\pi_0}(r)=\{\pi\in B_{K,c}(r):U(\pi)=U(\pi_0)\}$. Four filters govern box integration:

1. *Fixed distortion, arbitrary union:* $\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(K)$ (`l.toFilterDistortion K c`), generated by $\{B_{K,c}(r):r\ \ell\text{-allowed}\}$, which forms a filter basis (`hasBasis_toFilterDistortion`, cited from the [Cousin subsection](#cousins-lemma-fine-tagged-partitions-of-a-box)).
2. *Fixed distortion, prescribed union:* $\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(K,\pi_0)=\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(K)\sqcap\mathcal P\{\pi:U(\pi)=U(\pi_0)\}$ (`l.toFilterDistortioniUnion K c π₀`), with basis $\{B_{K,c,\pi_0}(r):r\ \ell\text{-allowed}\}$ (`hasBasis_toFilterDistortioniUnion`, cited from the Cousin subsection).
3. *All distortions, arbitrary union:* $\mathcal F_\ell(K)=\bigsqcup_{c\ge 0}\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(K)$ (`l.toFilter K`). By the supremum rule,
   $$S\in\mathcal F_\ell(K)\iff \forall c\ge 0,\ \exists r\ (\ell\text{-allowed}),\ B_{K,c}(r)\subseteq S .$$
   Choosing one allowed gauge $r_c$ for each $c\ge 0$ (`hasBasis_iSup`) gives the basis (`hasBasis_toFilter`) indexed by families $r=(r_c)_{c\ge 0}$ of $\ell$-allowed gauges, with basis sets
   $$\mathcal B_K(r)=\bigcup_{c\ge 0}B_{K,c}(r_c)=\bigl\{\pi:\exists c\ge 0,\ \pi\in B_{K,c}(r_c)\bigr\} .$$
4. *All distortions, prescribed union:* $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)=\bigsqcup_{c\ge 0}\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(K,\pi_0)$ (`l.toFilteriUnion K π₀`). Distributing the supremum over the principal filter (`iSup_inf_principal`) gives
   $$\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)=\mathcal F_\ell(K)\sqcap\mathcal P\{\pi:U(\pi)=U(\pi_0)\} ,$$
   which depends on $\pi_0$ only through its half-open union $U(\pi_0)$ (`toFilteriUnion_congr`). Its basis (`hasBasis_toFilteriUnion`) over families $r=(r_c)_{c\ge 0}$ of $\ell$-allowed gauges consists of the sets
   $$\mathcal B_{K,\pi_0}(r)=\bigl\{\pi:\exists c\ge 0,\ \pi\in B_{K,c}(r_c)\ \text{and}\ U(\pi)=U(\pi_0)\bigr\} .$$
   When $\pi_0=\top=\{K\}$ is the one-box partition of $K$, the condition $U(\pi)=K$ is `π.IsPartition`, giving the partition basis $\mathcal B_{K,\top}(r)$ (`hasBasis_toFilteriUnion_top`). By Corollary (c) of the Cousin subsection (using exact complements from the [split-complement subsection](#exact-complements-by-splitting-half-open-boxes)), both $\mathcal F_\ell(K)$ and $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ are nontrivial (`NeBot`). [Four filters and bases](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Filter.lean#L294-L325) · [Supremum bases](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Filter.lean#L431-L464)

A vector $y\in V$ is the *$\ell$-integral* of $f$ on $K$ (`HasIntegral K l f vol y`) if $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)$ converges to $y$ along $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top)$, meaning $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))\le\mathcal N(y)$. We say that $f$ is *$\ell$-integrable* on $K$ (`Integrable K l f vol`) if such a $y$ exists; `integral K l f vol`, written $\int_K f$, selects that unique $y$ (`HasIntegral.unique`, `HasIntegral.integral_eq`, cited from the Cousin subsection) and returns $0$ if $f$ is not integrable. A filter $\mathcal G$ on $V$ is *Cauchy* (`Cauchy`) if $\mathcal G$ is nontrivial (`NeBot`) and $\mathcal G\times^s\mathcal G\le\mathcal U(V)$, where $\mathcal U(V)$ is the metric uniformity generated by $U_\varepsilon=\{(v_1,v_2)\in V\times V:\lVert v_1-v_2\rVert\le\varepsilon\}$ ($\varepsilon>0$); equivalently, $\mathcal G$ is nontrivial and for every $\varepsilon>0$ some $T\in\mathcal G$ has diameter at most $\varepsilon$. The space $V$ is *complete* (`CompleteSpace V`) if every Cauchy filter $\mathcal G$ on $V$ satisfies $\mathcal G\le\mathcal N(y)$ for some $y\in V$. [Integral definitions](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L165-L178) · [Cauchy and complete spaces](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L27-L57) · [`CompleteSpace`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L367-L373)

**Interface from (N6a).** The [epsilon–gauge subsection](#from-the-box-filter-to-epsilon-and-gauges) proves the $\varepsilon$–gauge equivalence `hasIntegral_iff` (`Basic.lean:201–205`).
$$\mathrm{HasIntegral}(K,\ell,f,\mathrm{vol},y)\iff \forall\varepsilon>0,\ \exists r=(r_c)_{c\ge 0},\ (\forall c,\ \ell.\mathrm{RCond}(r_c))\land\forall c\ \pi,\ \pi\in B_{K,c,\top}(r_c)\implies\lVert S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)-y\rVert\le\varepsilon ,$$
The dependency in the subbox and Henstock–Sacks development is strictly one-way: **only the forward implication** (`hasIntegral_iff.1`), wrapped as `Integrable.convergenceR` (`Basic.lean:417–432`), is called, first on $K$ to extract $r_f(\varepsilon/2,\cdot)$ in the Cauchy step below, and later on $J$ in Step 1 of the Henstock–Sacks proof once subbox integrability on $J$ has already been established directly by filter composition. Neither `to_subbox` nor the Cauchy criterion below invokes the reverse implication `hasIntegral_iff.2`.

**Lemma 1 (subbox embedding and filter continuity).** [Lean, read], citing (N6c) for the complement prepartition. *Let $J\subseteq K$ be boxes ($J\le K$), and write $\pi_0=\{J\}$ (`Prepartition.single K J hJ`) for the one-cell untagged prepartition of $K$ with union $J$, and $\pi_0^c$ (`π₀.compl`) for its exact complement prepartition on $K\setminus J$.*

1. *(`TaggedPrepartition.embedBox`.) Sending each tagged prepartition $\pi$ of $J$ to the family $\iota_{J,K}(\pi)$ with the **same** finite set of cells and the **same** tag map defines an injection $\iota_{J,K}:\mathrm{TaggedPrepartition}(J)\hookrightarrow\mathrm{TaggedPrepartition}(K)$ satisfying $U(\iota_{J,K}(\pi))=U(\pi)$ and*
   $$S\bigl(f,\mathrm{vol},\iota_{J,K}(\pi)\bigr)=S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)$$
   *identically for every $f$ and $\mathrm{vol}$.*
2. *(`tendsto_embedBox_toFilteriUnion_top`.) For every parameter set $\ell$, $\iota_{J,K}$ is filter-continuous from full partitions of $J$ to ambient prepartitions of $K$ covering $J$:*
   $$\mathrm{Tendsto}\bigl(\iota_{J,K},\ \mathcal F_\ell(J,\top),\ \mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)\bigr) .$$
   *More explicitly, the quantifier and distortion shift are:*
   $$\forall c\ge 0,\ \exists c'\ge 0\ \bigl(\text{namely }c'=\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))\bigr),\ \forall r\ (\ell\text{-allowed}),\ \forall\pi\in B_{J,c,\top}(r),\quad \iota_{J,K}(\pi)\in B_{K,c',\pi_0}(r) .$$

*Proof.* (1) Every cell $B\in\pi$ satisfies $B\subseteq J\subseteq K$, its pairwise disjointness in $\mathbb R^n$ does not refer to the ambient box, and its tag lies in $\bar J\subseteq\bar K$ because $J\subseteq K$ implies $\bar J\subseteq\bar K$ (`Box.le_iff_Icc`). Thus $\iota_{J,K}(\pi)$ is a valid tagged prepartition of $K$. Since `embedBox` leaves both `.boxes` and `.tag` definitionally unchanged, injectivity is immediate and $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\iota_{J,K}(\pi))$ reduces definitionally to $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)$.

(2) Because $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top)=\bigsqcup_{c\ge 0}\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(J,\top)$, `tendsto_iSup` reduces $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top).\mathrm{map}(\iota_{J,K})\le\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ to proving, for each fixed source distortion level $c\ge 0$, that $\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(J,\top).\mathrm{map}(\iota_{J,K})\le\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$. Set $c'=\max\bigl(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c)\bigr)$. Since $\mathcal F_{\ell,c'}(K,\pi_0)\le\bigsqcup_{b\ge 0}\mathcal F_{\ell,b}(K,\pi_0)=\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ (`le_iSup_of_le`), it suffices to show $\mathrm{Tendsto}\bigl(\iota_{J,K},\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(J,\top),\mathcal F_{\ell,c'}(K,\pi_0)\bigr)$. Using the fixed-level bases $B_{J,c,\top}(r)$ and $B_{K,c',\pi_0}(r)$, fix an $\ell$-allowed gauge $r$ for $\mathcal F_{\ell,c'}(K,\pi_0)$. Because `l.RCond r` does not depend on the box, the **same** gauge $r$ is $\ell$-allowed for $\mathcal F_{\ell,c}(J,\top)$. Take any $\pi\in B_{J,c,\top}(r)$, so $\pi$ is $(c,r)$-admissible on $J$ and $U(\pi)=J$. Then $U(\iota_{J,K}(\pi))=U(\pi)=J=U(\pi_0)$ (`Prepartition.iUnion_single`). For `(c',r)`-admissibility on $K$:
- (A1) and (A2) depend only on individual cells, their closures and their tags, so they carry over unchanged from $\pi$.
- (A3) If $\mathtt{bDistortion}=\mathrm{true}$, then $\kappa(\iota_{J,K}(\pi))=\kappa(\pi)\le c\le\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))=c'$.
- (A4) Since $\pi$ partitions the **whole** subbox $J$, the complement of $U(\iota_{J,K}(\pi))$ inside $K$ is $K\setminus U(\pi)=K\setminus J=U(\pi_0^c)$ by (N6c), and $\kappa(\pi_0^c)\le\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))=c'$. So $\pi_0^c$ witnesses (A4) on $K$ at level $c'$.

Hence $\iota_{J,K}(\pi)\in B_{K,c',\pi_0}(r)$, completing the proof. In dual filter-set terms, if $S\in\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ is witnessed at every level $b\ge 0$ by $B_{K,b,\pi_0}(r_b)\subseteq S$, then $\iota_{J,K}^{-1}(S)\in\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top)$ is witnessed at source level $c$ by evaluating the ambient gauge family at the shifted index $q_c:=r_{\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))}$. $\square$ [`embedBox`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Tagged.lean#L345-L352) · [`tendsto_embedBox_toFilteriUnion_top`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Partition/Filter.lean#L466-L480)

**Lemma 2 (Cauchy filters and sequential completeness of Banach targets).** [Lean, read] *Let $V$ be a real normed space (or any metric space).*

1. *(`cauchy_map_iff'`, `cauchy_map_iff_exists_tendsto`.) Let $\mathcal L$ be a nontrivial filter (`NeBot`) on a set $X$ and $\Phi:X\to V$ any map. Then $\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi)$ is Cauchy if and only if $(\Phi\times\Phi)$ sends $\mathcal L\times^s\mathcal L$ into $\mathcal U(V)$. If $V$ is complete (`CompleteSpace V`), then*
   $$\mathrm{Cauchy}\bigl(\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi)\bigr)\iff \exists y\in V,\ \mathrm{Tendsto}(\Phi,\mathcal L,\mathcal N(y)) .$$
2. *(`SequentiallyComplete`, `UniformSpace.complete_of_cauchySeq_tendsto`.) If every Cauchy sequence in $V$ converges in $V$, then $V$ is complete in the filter sense (`CompleteSpace V`): **every** Cauchy filter $\mathcal G$ on $V$ converges to a point of $V$, even when $\mathcal G=\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi)$ comes from a filter $\mathcal L$ that has no countable basis.*

*Proof.* (1) Since $\mathcal L$ is nontrivial, $\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi)$ is automatically nontrivial (`map_neBot_iff`). By $(\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi))\times^s(\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi))=(\mathcal L\times^s\mathcal L).\mathrm{map}(\Phi\times\Phi)$ (`prod_map_map_eq`), the second clause of `Cauchy` is literally $\mathrm{Tendsto}(\Phi\times\Phi,\mathcal L\times^s\mathcal L,\mathcal U(V))$. If `CompleteSpace V` holds and $\mathcal G=\mathcal L.\mathrm{map}(\Phi)$ is Cauchy, `CompleteSpace.complete` gives $y\in V$ with $\mathcal G\le\mathcal N(y)$, which is $\mathrm{Tendsto}(\Phi,\mathcal L,\mathcal N(y))$. Conversely, for any $y\in V$, $\mathcal N(y)$ is Cauchy (`cauchy_nhds`, since $\bar B(y,\varepsilon/2)\times\bar B(y,\varepsilon/2)\subseteq U_\varepsilon$), and any nontrivial filter finer than a Cauchy filter is Cauchy (`Cauchy.mono`, `Filter.Tendsto.cauchy_map`).

(2) The metric uniformity $\mathcal U(V)$ has the countable antitone basis $U^{(n)}=\{(u,v)\in V\times V:\lVert u-v\rVert\le 2^{-n}\}$ ($n\in\mathbb N$; in `Cauchy.lean:917–921` Mathlib extracts a countable antitone basis from `IsCountablyGenerated (𝓤 V)`). Let $\mathcal G$ be any Cauchy filter on $V$. For each $n\in\mathbb N$, the Cauchy property of $\mathcal G$ gives a set $A_n\in\mathcal G$ with $A_n\times A_n\subseteq U^{(n)}$ (`setSeqAux`). Put (`setSeq`, `setSeq_mem`)
$$S_n=\bigcap_{m=0}^n A_m\in\mathcal G ,$$
using closure of $\mathcal G$ under finite intersections. Then $S_0\supseteq S_1\supseteq\cdots$ (`setSeq_mono`), and whenever $m,n\ge N$ we have $S_m\times S_n\subseteq S_N\times S_N\subseteq A_N\times A_N\subseteq U^{(N)}$ (`setSeq_prod_subset`). Because $\mathcal G$ is nontrivial, every $S_n$ is nonempty; choose one vector $y_n\in S_n\subseteq V$ (`seq`, `seq_mem`). For $m,n\ge N$ we have $(y_m,y_n)\in S_m\times S_n\subseteq U^{(N)}$, so $\lVert y_m-y_n\rVert\le 2^{-N}$. Thus $(y_n)_{n\ge 0}$ is an ordinary Cauchy sequence **in the metric space $V$** (`seq_is_cauchySeq`), and sequential completeness of $V$ supplies $y\in V$ with $y_n\to y$.

To prove that the **entire filter** $\mathcal G$ converges to $y$ ($\mathcal G\le\mathcal N(y)$, `le_nhds_of_seq_tendsto_nhds` via `le_nhds_of_cauchy_adhp_aux`), fix any $\varepsilon>0$. Choose $m\in\mathbb N$ with $2^{-m}\le\varepsilon/2$ and choose $n\in\mathbb N$ such that $\lVert y_k-y\rVert\le 2^{-m}$ for all $k\ge n$. Set $M=\max(m,n)$. Then $S_M\in\mathcal G$, the single sample point $y_M$ belongs to $S_M$ with $\lVert y_M-y\rVert\le 2^{-m}$ (since $M\ge n$), and for **every** $z\in S_M$ we have $(y_M,z)\in S_M\times S_M\subseteq U^{(m)}$ (since $M\ge m$), so
$$\lVert z-y\rVert\le\lVert z-y_M\rVert+\lVert y_M-y\rVert\le 2^{-m}+2^{-m}\le\varepsilon .$$
Hence $S_M\subseteq\bar B(y,\varepsilon)$, so $\bar B(y,\varepsilon)\in\mathcal G$. Since $\varepsilon>0$ was arbitrary, $\mathcal G\le\mathcal N(y)$. $\square$ [`cauchy_map_iff'` and `cauchy_map_iff_exists_tendsto`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L64-L87) · [Equivalence under `CompleteSpace`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L424-L431) · [`SequentiallyComplete` to `CompleteSpace`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean#L830-L921)

Observe what Step (2) did and did not do. When $\mathcal G=\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))$, the sequence $y_n=S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_n)$ samples one prepartition from each $S_n$ *solely to locate the candidate vector $y\in V$*. Convergence of $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)$ to $y$ holds on the full filter member $\Phi^{-1}(S_M)\in\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ — a set containing a gauge-basis set — because $S_M$ already has diameter at most $2^{1-m}$ by the full filter Cauchy hypothesis. Failure example 2 below shows that sequential convergence of $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_n)$ without the filter Cauchy hypothesis proves nothing about box integrability.

**Theorem (prepartition Cauchy nets, integrability on subboxes, and the Cauchy criterion).** [Lean, read], with Parts (1)–(2) using the cited Lemma 1 of the Henstock–Sacks subsection (hence (N6b) and (N6c)), cited filter nontriviality and limit uniqueness, and the forward `convergenceR` extraction from (N6a); Part (3) uses only the filter basis of $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top)$, cited nontriviality, and Lemma 2(1). *Let $K$ be a box in $\mathbb R^n$, let $\ell$ be an integration parameter, let $f:\mathbb R^n\to E$, and let $\mathrm{vol}$ be a box-additive operator-valued volume into a real normed space $V$.*

1. *(`tendsto_integralSum_toFilter_prod_self_inf_iUnion_eq_uniformity`, `cauchy_map_integralSum_toFilteriUnion` — no completeness assumed.) If $f$ is $\ell$-integrable on $K$, then $(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_1),S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_2))\to\mathcal U(V)$ along $\bigl(\mathcal F_\ell(K)\times^s\mathcal F_\ell(K)\bigr)\sqcap\mathcal P\{(\pi_1,\pi_2):U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)\}$. Consequently, for every untagged prepartition $\pi_0$ of $K$, the image filter $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))$ is Cauchy in $V$.*
2. *(`to_subbox_aux`, `to_subbox`, `tendsto_integralSum_toFilteriUnion_single` — closing (N6d).) Assume in addition that $V$ is complete (`CompleteSpace V`), and let $J\subseteq K$ be a subbox. If $f$ is $\ell$-integrable on $K$, then $f$ is $\ell$-integrable on $J$, and the ambient prepartition sums converge to $\int_J f$:*
   $$\mathrm{Tendsto}\bigl(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot),\ \mathcal F_\ell(K,\{J\}),\ \mathcal N\Bigl(\int_J f\Bigr)\bigr) .$$
3. *(`integrable_iff_cauchy`, `integrable_iff_cauchy_basis`.) Assume that $V$ is complete (`CompleteSpace V`). Then $f$ is $\ell$-integrable on $K$ if and only if $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))$ is Cauchy, if and only if:*
   $$\forall\varepsilon>0,\ \exists r:\mathbb R_{\ge 0}\to\mathbb R^n\to(0,\infty),\ \bigl(\forall c,\ \ell.\mathrm{RCond}(r_c)\bigr)\ \land$$
   $$\forall c_1,c_2\ge 0,\ \forall\pi_1,\pi_2,\quad \pi_1\in B_{K,c_1,\top}(r_{c_1})\implies \pi_2\in B_{K,c_2,\top}(r_{c_2})\implies \lVert S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_1)-S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_2)\rVert\le\varepsilon .$$

*Proof.* (1) Fix $\varepsilon>0$. Since $\varepsilon/2>0$, the forward clause `convergenceR` of (N6a) applied to `h : Integrable K l f vol` gives an $\ell$-allowed gauge family $r=r_f(\varepsilon/2,\cdot)$ on $K$ (`h.convergenceR_cond (ε / 2)`). In the product-principal basis of $\bigl(\mathcal F_\ell(K)\times^s\mathcal F_\ell(K)\bigr)\sqcap\mathcal P\{U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)\}$ built from `hasBasis_toFilter`, take any pair $(\pi_1,\pi_2)\in(\mathcal B_K(r)\times\mathcal B_K(r))\cap\{U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)\}$. By definition of $\mathcal B_K(r)$, there exist **possibly distinct** levels $c_1,c_2\ge 0$ such that $\pi_1\in B_{K,c_1}(r_f(\varepsilon/2,c_1))$, $\pi_2\in B_{K,c_2}(r_f(\varepsilon/2,c_2))$ and $U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)$. Lemma 1 of the Henstock–Sacks subsection (`dist_integralSum_le_of_memBaseSet`, at $\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon_2=\varepsilon/2$) yields
$$\lVert S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_1)-S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_2)\rVert\le\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon ,$$
proving the uniformity limit. Now fix an untagged prepartition $\pi_0$ of $K$. By `toFilter_inf_iUnion_eq`, $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)=\mathcal F_\ell(K)\sqcap\mathcal P\{U(\cdot)=U(\pi_0)\}$, so (`prod_inf_prod`, `prod_principal_principal`)
$$\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)\times^s\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)=\bigl(\mathcal F_\ell(K)\times^s\mathcal F_\ell(K)\bigr)\sqcap\mathcal P\bigl\{(\pi_1,\pi_2):U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_0)\ \text{and}\ U(\pi_2)=U(\pi_0)\bigr\} .$$
Whenever $U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_0)$ and $U(\pi_2)=U(\pi_0)$, transitivity gives $U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)$, so this product filter is finer than $\bigl(\mathcal F_\ell(K)\times^s\mathcal F_\ell(K)\bigr)\sqcap\mathcal P\{U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)\}$. Since $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ is nontrivial (`toFilteriUnion_neBot`), Lemma 2(1) shows that $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))$ is Cauchy.

(2) Apply Part (1) to $\pi_0=\{J\}$ (`Prepartition.single K J hJ`). Since $V$ is complete, Lemma 2(1) (`cauchy_map_iff_exists_tendsto.1`) produces $y\in V$ with
$$\mathrm{Tendsto}\bigl(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot),\ \mathcal F_\ell(K,\{J\}),\ \mathcal N(y)\bigr) .$$
Composing on the right with Lemma 1(2) (`hy.comp (l.tendsto_embedBox_toFilteriUnion_top hJ)`) gives
$$\mathrm{Tendsto}\bigl(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)\circ\iota_{J,K},\ \mathcal F_\ell(J,\top),\ \mathcal N(y)\bigr) .$$
By Lemma 1(1), $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)\circ\iota_{J,K}=S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)$ identically (`convert!` in `Basic.lean:501–503`), so the displayed limit is literally $\mathrm{HasIntegral}(J,\ell,f,\mathrm{vol},y)$. This proves `to_subbox_aux`: $f$ is $\ell$-integrable on $J$ (`to_subbox`), and uniqueness on $J$ (`h₁.integral_eq`) identifies $y=\int_J f$, giving `tendsto_integralSum_toFilteriUnion_single`.

(3) Because $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top)$ is nontrivial (`toFilteriUnion_neBot`), Lemma 2(1) immediately gives `integrable_iff_cauchy`: $\mathrm{Integrable}(K,\ell,f,\mathrm{vol})\iff\mathrm{Cauchy}\bigl(\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot))\bigr)$. Rewriting `cauchy_map_iff'` with `(l.hasBasis_toFilteriUnion_top K).prod_self.tendsto_iff uniformity_basis_dist_le` expresses the right-hand side as: for every $\varepsilon>0$ there exists an $\ell$-allowed family $r=(r_c)_{c\ge 0}$ such that every $(\pi_1,\pi_2)\in\mathcal B_{K,\top}(r)\times\mathcal B_{K,\top}(r)$ satisfies $\lVert S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_1)-S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi_2)\rVert\le\varepsilon$. Unpacking $\pi_i\in\mathcal B_{K,\top}(r)$ as $\exists c_i\ge 0,\ \pi_i\in B_{K,c_i,\top}(r_{c_i})$ and commuting $\exists c_1,\exists c_2$ out of the antecedent as $\forall c_1,c_2\ge 0$ (`Basic.lean:231–235`) yields the stated criterion. $\square$ [Cauchy criterion](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L218-L236) · [Prepartition Cauchy net and subboxes](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L473-L516)

**Corollary (prepartition limits and box additivity of the indefinite integral).** [Lean, read], using the Henstock–Sacks theorem now that Part (2) has discharged (N6d). *Assume `CompleteSpace V` and let $f$ be $\ell$-integrable on $K$.*

1. *(`tendsto_integralSum_sum_integral`.) For every untagged prepartition $\pi_0$ of $K$, $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)$ converges along $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ to $\sum_{J\in\pi_0}\int_J f$.*
2. *(`sum_integral_congr`, `Integrable.toBoxAdditive`.) If $\pi_1,\pi_2$ are untagged prepartitions of $K$ with $U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)$, then $\sum_{J\in\pi_1}\int_J f=\sum_{J\in\pi_2}\int_J f$. In particular, $J\mapsto\int_J f$ defines a box-additive map (`h.toBoxAdditive`) on subboxes $J\subseteq K$: for every untagged partition $\pi$ of $J$, $\sum_{B\in\pi}\int_B f=\int_J f$.*

*Proof.* (1) Using `hasBasis_toFilteriUnion` against closed balls $\bar B(\sum_{J\in\pi_0}\int_J f,\varepsilon)$, fix $\varepsilon>0$ and take the $\ell$-allowed gauge family $r=r_f(\varepsilon,\cdot)$ on $K$. Every $\pi\in\mathcal B_{K,\pi_0}(r)$ satisfies $\pi\in B_{K,c}(r_f(\varepsilon,c))$ for some $c\ge 0$ and $U(\pi)=U(\pi_0)$, so the Henstock–Sacks theorem (`dist_integralSum_sum_integral_le_of_memBaseSet_of_iUnion_eq`) bounds $\lVert S(f,\mathrm{vol},\pi)-\sum_{J\in\pi_0}\int_J f\rVert\le\varepsilon$. (2) If $U(\pi_1)=U(\pi_2)$, then $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_1)=\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_2)$ (`toFilteriUnion_congr`). Part (1) gives convergence of $S(f,\mathrm{vol},\cdot)$ along this common nontrivial filter to both sums, and Hausdorff uniqueness (`tendsto_nhds_unique`) equates them. For $J\subseteq K$, Part (2) of the theorem gives integrability of $f$ on $J$; applying `sum_integral_congr` on $J$ to an untagged partition $\pi$ of $J$ and $\top=\{J\}$ (both having union $J$) yields $\sum_{B\in\pi}\int_B f=\int_J f$. $\square$ [Prepartition limit and `toBoxAdditive`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L598-L631)

**How Henstock–Sacks and the divergence chain consume these results.** [Lean, read] Two distinct paths in §3.4.1 spend the theorem and its corollary, and both rely on `CompleteSpace`:

| Consumer site | Exact call in pinned Mathlib | Input spent and distortion / completeness handling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Henstock–Sacks Step 1 (`Basic.lean:546–559`) | `HJi : Integrable J l f vol := h.to_subbox Hle`, then `min (h.convergenceR δ' C x) (HJi.convergenceR δ' C x)` | Spends Theorem Part (2) (`[CompleteSpace F]`) on each $J\in\pi_0$, then calls `(N6a)`'s forward `convergenceR` on $J$ at the pre-chosen level $C=\max(\kappa(\pi_0),\kappa(\pi_0^c))$; feeds McShane–Bochner comparison (`Integrability.lean:261–285`). |
| Continuous face traces (`Basic.lean:645–654, 764–774`) | `integrable_iff_cauchy_basis.2` $\to$ `integrable_of_bounded_and_ae_continuousWithinAt` $\to$ `integrable_of_continuousOn` | Spends Theorem Part (3) (`[CompleteSpace E]`). Continuity on compact $\bar I$ gives boundedness (`isCompact_Icc.image_of_continuousOn`) and everywhere continuity within $\bar I$; the Cauchy criterion is discharged with a constant gauge $r/2$ independent of $c_1,c_2$. |
| GP partial-derivative flux (`BoxIntegral/DivergenceTheorem.lean:61, 87–89, 169–171, 204–208`) | `fb := fun x => (integrable_of_continuousOn GP … volume).toBoxAdditive`, fed to `BoxAdditiveMap.upperSubLower` | Spends the Corollary (`toBoxAdditive`) at $\ell=\mathrm{GP}$ on each codimension-one face `I.face i` (`[CompleteSpace E]` at line 61) so that upper-minus-lower face integrals form a box-additive map on subboxes of $I$. |
| Bochner divergence to Cauchy–Goursat (`MeasureTheory/Integral/DivergenceTheorem.lean:118–136`) | `wlog hE : CompleteSpace E` (`simp [integral, hE]` if false), then calls GP divergence and McShane comparison | Discharges incomplete $E$ because both sides of the stated equality are Bochner integrals and reduce to $0$ there, then supplies `[CompleteSpace E]` to both chains. In rectangle Cauchy–Goursat, $E=\mathbb C$ is complete. |

**Hand computation 10 (one dimension, complex boundary spike, nonconstant Henstock gauge).** Take $K=(0,2]$, $J=(0,1]$, and $F:\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$ given by $F(x)=\mathbf 1_{x\le 1/2}+4i\,\mathbf 1_{x=1}$ at $\ell=\mathrm{Henstock}$ (which coincides with $\mathrm{GP}$ in dimension one because every interval has distortion $1$, and $\pi_0=\{J\}$ has complement $\pi_0^c=\{(1,2]\}$ of distortion $1$, so $\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))=\max(c,1)$). Here $\int_K F=\int_J F=1/2$ and $\int_{(1,2]}F=0$.

*A verified nonconstant convergence gauge.* For $\eta>0$, define
$$r_\eta(x)=\begin{cases}\eta/16 & (x=1),\\\eta/8 & (x\ne 1).\end{cases}$$
Let $\pi$ be any $r_\eta$-fine Henstock prepartition of $K$. In the real part $u(x)=\mathbf 1_{x\le 1/2}$, any cell $B=(a,b]$ with tag $t_B\in[a,b]$ has $\lvert q-t_B\rvert\le r_\eta(t_B)\le\eta/8$ for all $q\in B$, so $u(t_B)\ne u(q)$ forces $\lvert q-1/2\rvert\le\eta/8$; by pairwise disjointness of the half-open cells, $\lvert\Re S(F,\pi)-\sum_{B\in\pi}\int_B u\rvert\le\lvert[1/2-\eta/8,1/2+\eta/8]\rvert=\eta/4$. In the imaginary part $4\mathbf 1_{x=1}$, only cells with $t_B=1$ contribute; any such Henstock cell lies in $[1-r_\eta(1),1+r_\eta(1)]=[1-\eta/16,1+\eta/16]$ (at most two cells, $(a,1]$ and $(1,b]$, can share the tag $1$), so their disjoint lengths sum to at most $2r_\eta(1)=\eta/8$ and $\lvert\Im S(F,\pi)\rvert\le 4(\eta/8)=\eta/2$. Hence
$$\Bigl\lvert S(F,\pi)-\sum_{B\in\pi}\int_B F\Bigr\rvert\le\frac{\eta}{4}+\frac{\eta}{2}=\frac{3}{4}\eta\le\eta ,$$
and in particular $r_F(\eta):=r_\eta$ is a valid convergence gauge for $F$ on $K$.

*Two embedded partitions of $J$ and their common completion at Cauchy tolerance $\varepsilon=1$.* Set $\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon_2=1/2$, so $\rho=r_F(1/2)$ has $\rho(x)=1/16$ for $x\ne 1$ and $\rho(1)=1/32$. Consider two $\rho$-fine Henstock partitions of $J=(0,1]$ and one $\rho$-fine Henstock partition $Q_c$ of $K\setminus J=(1,2]$:
- $\pi_1$ ($8$ cells): $\bigl(\frac{k-1}{8},\frac{k}{8}\bigr]$ tagged at its midpoint $\frac{2k-1}{16}$ for $k=1,\dots,8$ (each radius is $1/16=\rho(t_k)$, and no tag is $1$).
- $\pi_2$ ($17$ cells): $\bigl(\frac{k-1}{16},\frac{k}{16}\bigr]$ tagged at its left endpoint $\frac{k-1}{16}$ for $k=1,\dots,15$ (radius $1/16$), together with $\bigl(\frac{15}{16},\frac{31}{32}\bigr]$ at $\frac{15}{16}$ (radius $1/32<\rho(\frac{15}{16})$) and $\bigl(\frac{31}{32},1\bigr]$ at $1$ (radius $1/32=\rho(1)$, sharp at the spike).
- $Q_c$ ($17$ cells on $(1,2]$): $\bigl(1,\frac{33}{32}\bigr]$ tagged at its left endpoint $1$ (outside $(1,\frac{33}{32}]$ yet inside $\overline{(1,\frac{33}{32}]}$, radius $1/32=\rho(1)$), $\bigl(\frac{33}{32},\frac{17}{16}\bigr]$ at $\frac{17}{16}$ (radius $1/32$), and $\bigl(\frac{16+j}{16},\frac{17+j}{16}\bigr]$ at its midpoint $\frac{33+2j}{32}$ for $j=1,\dots,15$ (radius $1/32$).

| Family | Domain covered | Cell count | Max radius at $t\ne 1$ / at $t=1$ | Tagged sum $S(F,\cdot)$ | Exact error vs. box integral |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $\pi_1$ (embedded in $K$) | $J=(0,1]$ | $8$ | $1/16$ / none | $1/2$ | $0$ |
| $\pi_2$ (embedded in $K$) | $J=(0,1]$ | $17$ | $1/16$ / $1/32$ | $9/16+i/8$ | $\lvert 1/16+i/8\rvert=\sqrt{5}/16$ |
| $Q_c$ | $K\setminus J=(1,2]$ | $17$ | $1/32$ / $1/32$ | $i/8$ | $\lvert i/8\rvert=1/8$ |
| $\pi_1\sqcup Q_c$ | $K=(0,2]$ | $25$ | $1/16$ / $1/32$ | $1/2+i/8$ | $1/8\le 3/8\le 1/2$ |
| $\pi_2\sqcup Q_c$ | $K=(0,2]$ | $34$ | $1/16$ / $1/32$ (two cells) | $9/16+i/4$ | $\lvert 1/16+i/4\rvert=\sqrt{17}/16<3/8\le 1/2$ |

In $\pi_2$, nine cells ($k=1,\dots,9$, including $(1/2,9/16]$ tagged at $1/2$) contribute $1/16$ to $\Re S$, while $(31/32,1]$ tagged at $1$ contributes $4i/32=i/8$. In $\pi_2\sqcup Q_c$, **both** adjacent cells $(31/32,1]$ and $(1,33/32]$ are tagged at $1$, doubling the imaginary error to $i/4$; yet subtracting the two completed sums cancels $S(F,Q_c)=i/8$ identically and gives $\lvert S(F,\pi_1)-S(F,\pi_2)\rvert=\sqrt{5}/16<1=\varepsilon$. Among uniform right-tagged partitions $P_N=\bigl\{\bigl(\frac{k-1}{N},\frac{k}{N}\bigr]\text{ at }\frac{k}{N}:1\le k\le N\bigr\}$ of $J$, cell $N$ is tagged at $1$ with radius $1/N$, so $P_N\in B_{J,1,\top}(\rho)$ holds **if and only if $N\ge 32$** (not $N\ge 16$), with $S(F,P_N)=\frac{\lfloor N/2\rfloor}{N}+\frac{4i}{N}\to\frac{1}{2}$. In Step 1 of the Henstock–Sacks proof at $\delta'=1/2$, pairing $r_F(1/2)=r_{1/2}$ on $K$ with a tighter convergence gauge such as $r_{1/4}$ on $J$ produces $\rho_J=\min(r_{1/2},r_{1/4})=r_{1/4}$, taking the values $1/32$ for $x\ne 1$ and $1/64$ at $x=1$.

**Hand computation 11 (two dimensions, GP parameter, two complement witnesses and level shifting).** Take $K=(0,2]\times(0,1]$ ($\kappa(K)=2$) and its corner square $J=(0,1/2]^2$ ($\kappa(J)=1$) at $\ell=\mathrm{GP}$, with $F:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb C$ defined by
$$F(x,y)=\mathbf 1_{x\le 1/4}+i\,\mathbf 1_{y\le 1/4} .$$
Then $\int_K F=\frac{1}{4}+\frac{i}{2}$, $\int_J F=\frac{1}{8}+\frac{i}{8}$ and $\int_{K\setminus J}F=\frac{1}{8}+\frac{3i}{8}$. For any constant gauge $r\equiv\rho>0$ and any $\rho$-fine prepartition $\pi$ of $K$, real-part mismatches occur only on $\{q\in K:\lvert q_x-1/4\rvert\le\rho\}$ (height $1$, area at most $2\rho$) and imaginary-part mismatches only on $\{q\in K:\lvert q_y-1/4\rvert\le\rho\}$ (width $2$, area at most $4\rho$), giving $\lvert S(F,\pi)-\sum_{B\in\pi}\int_B F\rvert\le 2\rho+4\rho=6\rho$. Hence $r_F(\eta,c)\equiv\eta/6$ is a valid GP convergence gauge on $K$ at every distortion level $c\ge 0$.

*Two exact-complement witnesses on $K\setminus J$ and their shifted levels.* Let $\pi_0=\{J\}$.
1. Cutting $K$ only along the faces $x=1/2$ and $y=1/2$ of $J$ (`N6c` Lemma 1) yields the three-box complement $D_{\mathrm{grid}}=\bigl\{(1/2,2]\times(0,1/2],\, (0,1/2]\times(1/2,1],\, (1/2,2]\times(1/2,1]\bigr\}$, whose cell distortions are $3,1,3$, so $\kappa(D_{\mathrm{grid}})=3$. With $\pi_0^c=D_{\mathrm{grid}}$, Lemma 1(2) shifts source levels by $c\mapsto\max(c,3)$: both $c=1$ and $c=2$ land at ambient level $c'=3$.
2. Adding the vertical cuts $x=1$ and $x=3/2$ refines $K\setminus J$ into the seven-square complement $D_{\mathrm{sq}}$ of side $1/2$, with $\kappa(D_{\mathrm{sq}})=1$; this witness keeps $c=1$ at $\max(1,1)=1$ and $c=2$ at $\max(2,1)=2$. Either witness suffices in $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP}}(K,\{J\})=\bigsqcup_{c'\ge 0}\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP},c'}(K,\{J\})$ because the supremum ranges over all $c'\ge 0$.

Fix Cauchy tolerance $\varepsilon=3/2$, so $\varepsilon_1=\varepsilon_2=3/4$ and $\rho_2=r_F(3/4,c)=1/8$ for every $c\ge 0$. Test three $\rho_2$-fine midpoint-tagged Henstock partitions of $J=(0,1/2]^2$:
- $p_1$: the $2\times 2$ grid of $4$ squares of side $1/4$;
- $p_2$: the $3\times 3$ grid of $9$ squares of side $1/6$ (six tags have $p_x\in\{1/12,3/12\}\subseteq[0,1/4]$ and six have $p_y\le 1/4$, each of area $1/36$);
- $p_3$: the $2\times 4$ grid of $8$ rectangles of width $1/4$ and height $1/8$ (distortion $2$).

Bisecting each of the $7$ squares of $D_{\mathrm{sq}}$ into $4$ squares of side $1/4$ with midpoint tags gives a $28$-cell Cousin completion $Q_{c,2}$ of $K\setminus J$ of radius $1/8=\rho_2$ and distortion $1$, on which $x=1/4$ and $y=1/4$ are grid lines so $S(F,Q_{c,2})=\int_{K\setminus J}F=\frac{1}{8}+\frac{3i}{8}$:

| Partition on $J$ | Cells | Radius | Source level $c$ on $J$ | Ambient level $c'$ ($D_{\mathrm{grid}}$ / $D_{\mathrm{sq}}$) | $S(F,p_i)$ | $S(F,p_i\sqcup Q_{c,2})$ | Error vs. $\int_J F$ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $p_1$ | $4$ | $1/8$ | $1$ | $3$ / $1$ | $\tfrac{1}{8}+\tfrac{i}{8}$ | $\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{i}{2}$ | $0$ |
| $p_2$ | $9$ | $1/12$ | $1$ | $3$ / $1$ | $\tfrac{1}{6}+\tfrac{i}{6}$ | $\tfrac{7}{24}+\tfrac{13i}{24}$ | $\tfrac{\sqrt{2}}{24}$ |
| $p_3$ | $8$ | $1/8$ | $2$ | $3$ / $2$ | $\tfrac{1}{8}+\tfrac{i}{8}$ | $\tfrac{1}{4}+\tfrac{i}{2}$ | $0$ |

Here $p_2$ and $p_3$ belong to $\mathcal B_{J,\top}(\rho_2)$ at **distinct** distortion levels $c_1=1$ and $c_2=2$, matching the two-level quantifier $\forall c_1,c_2\ge 0$ of Theorem Part (3), and $\lvert S(F,p_2)-S(F,p_3)\rvert=\lvert(S(F,p_2)+S(F,Q_{c,2}))-(S(F,p_3)+S(F,Q_{c,2}))\rvert=\sqrt{2}/24<3/2=\varepsilon$.

**Failure examples when completeness or quantifier discipline is dropped.**

1. *Deleting `CompleteSpace V` breaks both `to_subbox` and the Cauchy criterion.* Let $V=(c_{00},\lVert\cdot\rVert_\infty)$ be the incomplete normed space of finitely supported real sequences $a=(a_1,a_2,\dots)$ under $\lVert a\rVert_\infty=\sup_{k\ge 1}\lvert a_k\rvert$, with standard basis vectors $e_k$. On $K=(0,2]$ and $J=(0,1]$, define $f:\mathbb R\to c_{00}$ by
   $$f(x)=\begin{cases}e_k & \text{if }x\in I_k^+=(2^{-k},2^{1-k}]\text{ for some }k\ge 1,\\ -e_k & \text{if }x\in I_k^-=(1+2^{-k},1+2^{1-k}]\text{ for some }k\ge 1,\\ 0 & \text{otherwise.}\end{cases}$$
   Since $\{I_k^+\}_{k\ge 1}$ partitions $(0,1]$ and $\{I_k^-\}_{k\ge 1}$ partitions $(1,2]$ into pairwise disjoint half-open intervals, $f(x)\in c_{00}$ at every point $x\in\mathbb R$, and every finite sum $S(f,\pi)$ lies in $c_{00}$.
   - *Integrability on $K=(0,2]$ with integral $0\in c_{00}$.* Fix $\delta>0$ and let $\pi$ be any $\delta$-fine tagged partition of $K$ (even without the Henstock condition). For each $k\ge 1$, every cell tagged in $I_k^+$ lies in $[2^{-k}-\delta,2^{1-k}+\delta]$, so disjointness gives $\sum_{p_B\in I_k^+}\lvert B\rvert\le 2^{-k}+2\delta$, and likewise $\sum_{p_B\in I_k^-}\lvert B\rvert\le 2^{-k}+2\delta$. If $2^{-k}\le 4\delta$, both sums lie in $[0,6\delta]$, so $\lvert(S(f,\pi))_k\rvert\le 6\delta$. If $2^{-k}>4\delta$, cells tagged outside $I_k^+$ lie in $(-\infty,2^{-k}+\delta]\cup[2^{1-k}-\delta,\infty)$ and therefore miss $(2^{-k}+\delta,2^{1-k}-\delta]\subseteq K$, forcing $\sum_{p_B\in I_k^+}\lvert B\rvert\ge 2^{-k}-2\delta$, and similarly $\sum_{p_B\in I_k^-}\lvert B\rvert\ge 2^{-k}-2\delta$; subtracting the two intervals $[2^{-k}-2\delta,2^{-k}+2\delta]$ gives $\lvert(S(f,\pi))_k\rvert\le 4\delta$. Taking $\delta=\varepsilon/8$ yields $\lVert S(f,\pi)-0\rVert_\infty\le 6\delta<\varepsilon$ for every $\delta$-fine partition $\pi$ of $K$. Thus $f$ is Riemann, Henstock, GP and McShane integrable on $K$ with $\int_K f=0$.
   - *Cauchy sums without an integral on $J=(0,1]$.* The same one-interval estimate on $I_k^+\subseteq J$ gives $\lvert(S(f,\pi))_k-2^{-k}\rvert\le 4\delta$ for every $k\ge 1$ and every $\delta$-fine tagged partition $\pi$ of $J$; on the dyadic right-tagged partitions $P_{2^m}$ of $J$, $(S(f,P_{2^m}))_k$ is $2^{-k}$ for $k\le m$, $2^{-m}$ for $k=m+1$, and $0$ for $k\ge m+2$, at $\ell^\infty$-distance $2^{-(m+1)}$ from $v=(2^{-1},2^{-2},\dots)$. Hence any two $\delta$-fine partitions $\pi_1,\pi_2$ of $J$ satisfy $\lVert S(f,\pi_1)-S(f,\pi_2)\rVert_{c_{00}}\le 8\delta$, so $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\cdot))$ is Cauchy in $c_{00}$ for all four parameter sets $\ell$ (confirming that Theorem Part (1) needs no completeness). Yet no $w\in c_{00}$ can be the $\ell$-integral of $f$ on $J$: choosing $K_0\ge 1$ with $w_{K_0}=0$, the $1$-Lipschitz coordinate projection $u\mapsto u_{K_0}$ would send $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top).\mathrm{map}(S(f,\cdot))$ to $\mathcal N(0)$ while it already converges to $2^{-K_0}\ne 0$, contradicting uniqueness of limits along the nontrivial filter $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top)$. The cancellation between $J$ and $K\setminus J$ kept $\int_K f=0$ inside $c_{00}$, whereas the subbox limit $v\in c_0\setminus c_{00}$ leaves the incomplete space.
2. *Sequential convergence of partitions is not convergence along $\mathcal F_\ell(J,\top)$, and quantifiers do not commute.* On $J=(0,1]$, let $g=\mathbf 1_{\mathbb Q\cap(0,1]}$. Along the uniform right-tagged partitions $P_N$ ($N\ge 1$), every tag $k/N$ is rational and $S(g,P_N)=1$ for all $N$, a convergent sequence; along the same cells with irrational tags $(k-1+\sqrt{2}/2)/N$, every sum is $0$. At $\ell=\mathrm{Riemann}$, every constant-gauge basis set $B_{J,1,\top}(\delta)$ contains members of both sequences once $1/N\le\delta$, so the filter image is not Cauchy despite convergence of $(S(g,P_N))_{N\ge 1}$. Even at $\ell=\mathrm{Henstock}$, where $g$ *is* integrable with $\int_J g=0$ by Lemma 3(b) of the Fubini subsection, the sequence $(P_N)_{N\ge 1}$ does **not** tend to $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{Henstock}}(J,\top)$: enumerating $\mathbb Q\cap[0,1]=\{q_1,q_2,\dots\}$ and setting $r^*(q_j)=2^{-j-2}$ on rationals and $r^*(x)=1$ on irrationals gives an allowed gauge for which every $\pi\in B_{J,1,\top}(r^*)$ satisfies $S(g,\pi)\le\sum_{j\ge 1}2r^*(q_j)=1/2<1$, so **no** $P_N$ ever enters the filter set $B_{J,1,\top}(r^*)$. Likewise, in `hasBasis_toFilteriUnion_top` the quantifier is $\forall c\ge 0,\ \exists r_c$; swapping it to $\exists r,\ \forall c\ge 0$ forces a single distortion-independent gauge and collapses `GP` into `Henstock` on full partitions. And in Lemma 1(2), swapping $\forall c\ge 0,\ \exists c'\ge 0$ to $\exists c'\ge 0,\ \forall c\ge 0$ fails already on $J=K=(0,1]^2$: the $k$ vertical strips $\bigl(\frac{j-1}{k},\frac{j}{k}\bigr]\times(0,1]$ tagged at $\bigl(\frac{2j-1}{2k},\frac{1}{2}\bigr)$ are $1/2$-fine Henstock partitions of distortion $\kappa=k$, which exceed any fixed $c'<k$.
3. *Why `embedBox` cannot preserve a fixed GP distortion level.* By Remark 2 of the [split-complement subsection](#exact-complements-by-splitting-half-open-boxes), for $K=(0,1+\sqrt{2}]\times(0,1]$ and $J=(0,1]^2$, the complement $K\setminus J=(1,1+\sqrt{2}]\times(0,1]$ has aspect ratio $\sqrt{2}>1$ and admits no finite tiling by squares. On $J$ itself, $\kappa(J)=1$ and the complement in $J$ is empty, so $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP},1}(J,\top)$ is nontrivial. On $K$, however, no prepartition with union $J$ satisfies (A4) at level $1$, so $B_{K,1,\{J\}}(r)=\varnothing$ for every gauge $r$ and $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP},1}(K,\{J\})=\bot$. Thus $\iota_{J,K}$ does **not** map $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP},1}(J,\top)$ into $\mathcal F_{\mathrm{GP},1}(K,\{J\})$. The one-cell complement gives the sufficient level $\sqrt{2}$. A grid of $m$ equal columns and $n$ equal rows in the complement has cell ratio of width to height $\sqrt{2}\,n/m$ and distortion $\max(\sqrt{2}\,n/m,m/(\sqrt{2}\,n))$. Positive rational approximations $m/n\to\sqrt{2}$ therefore give a completion at every prescribed level $c>1$.

**Remarks.**

1. *Reading scope and totalized integrals.* The [epsilon–gauge subsection](#from-the-box-filter-to-epsilon-and-gauges) reads (N6a). The rectangle integration supply still uses (N1)–(N5) and the named general filter and uniform-space inputs below. The box integral defaults to $0$ on nonintegrable functions. In `MeasureTheory/Integral/DivergenceTheorem.lean:118–120` the branch for an incomplete target instead simplifies both Bochner integrals in the stated equality to $0$ by the definition of the Bochner integral (`Bochner/Basic.lean:156–158`).
2. *Literature comparison.* Fremlin defines gauge integrals as limits of finite Riemann sums along a filter of $\delta$-fine, $\mathcal R$-filling tagged partitions in *Measure Theory*, Vol. 4, §§481A–481F [Fremlin2003Ch48, pp. 1–4], and in his real-valued Saks–Henstock proof §482B(b) [Fremlin2003Ch48, pp. 13–14] he constructs the indefinite integral $F(E)$ on the generated set algebra by swapping complementary pieces to bound oscillation along an algebra-aligned filter $\mathcal F^*$ by $2\varepsilon$, concluding from $\liminf=\limsup$ in $\mathbb R$ [Paper]. In his vector-valued note [Fremlin2010, §1B, §1D(b), pp. 2–3], the same $2\varepsilon$ oscillation bound along $\mathcal F^*$ explicitly invokes completeness of the target Banach space $W$ to define $F(E)\in W$ [Paper]. Those abstract frameworks allow residual gaps in $\mathcal R$ and align one filter $\mathcal F^*$ to all finite Boolean algebras at once; Kudryashov's pinned Mathlib proof works instead with exact half-open box unions, one filter $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\pi_0)$ per union, and an explicit supremum over GP distortion levels $c\ge 0$ bridged on subboxes by the shift $c\mapsto\max(c,\kappa(\pi_0^c))$ [Expository].
3. *Sources and reading level.* `Partition/Filter.lean`, `Partition/Tagged.lean`, `Partition/Additive.lean`, `Basic.lean` and `BoxIntegral/DivergenceTheorem.lean` are by Yury Kudryashov; `Topology/UniformSpace/Cauchy.lean` is by Johannes Hölzl and Mario Carneiro. #### From the box filter to epsilon and gauges

**Motivation.** The Henstock–Sacks argument completes two tagged pieces to full partitions. To estimate each completed sum it needs a gauge at the distortion level of that completion. The box integral starts with a single filter limit. We will unpack that limit and recover the exact quantifiers needed to choose these gauges.

**Example.** Divide $(0,1]$ into $N$ equal intervals and tag each at its right endpoint. For $f(t)=t$, the tagged sum is $(N+1)/(2N)$. Every cell has distortion $1$. In the plane, a rectangle of width $1/(mN)$ and height $1/N$ has distortion $m$ even for very large $N$. Thus size and distortion play different roles in the fineness condition.

**Definition.** Fix a box $K$ in a finite-dimensional coordinate space $\mathbb R^\iota$, integration parameters $\ell$, real normed spaces $E,V$, a function $f:\mathbb R^\iota\to E$, and a box-additive volume with values $\mathrm{vol}(J):E\to V$ in the continuous linear maps. Write $S(\pi)=\sum_{J\in\pi}\mathrm{vol}(J)(f(t_J))$. Tags $t_J$ belong to the closed ambient box. A gauge $r$ is a strictly positive real function on $\mathbb R^\iota$. Write $R_\ell(r)$ for the admissibility condition on gauges, which requires constancy when the Riemann flag is on.

Write $B_c(r)$ for the set of **full** tagged partitions satisfying $\mathrm{MemBaseSet}$ at $(c,r)$, for $c\in[0,\infty)$. Each cell is contained in the closed sup-norm ball of radius $r(t_J)$ about $t_J$. If the Henstock flag is on, $t_J$ belongs to the closure of its cell. If the distortion flag is on, the maximum ratio of side lengths over the cells is at most $c$, and there must be an untagged complementary prepartition with distortion at most $c$. For full partitions the empty prepartition witnesses the latter clause and has distortion $0$. We use the same $\mathrm{MemBaseSet}$ conditions on prepartitions, with their actual uncovered region. [Pinned definitions][ch3-n6a-filter-def]

**Lemma (the full-partition basis).** [Lean, read]. A subset $A$ of the tagged prepartitions belongs to $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top)$ if and only if there exists a family $(r_c)_{c\ge0}$ with $R_\ell(r_c)$ for every $c$ and
$$\bigcup_{c\ge0} B_c(r_c)\subseteq A.$$

*Proof.* Before requiring full coverage, the fixed-level filter is an infimum of principal filters on the sets defined by $\mathrm{MemBaseSet}$ for allowed gauges. The constant gauge $1$ is allowed. For two allowed gauges their pointwise minimum is allowed and its set is contained in both sets, since fineness strengthens while all other clauses are unchanged. Hence these sets form a basis of the infimum: their supersets form a filter refining every listed principal filter, and every filter refining those principal filters contains all the supersets. Meeting with the principal filter of full coverage intersects each basis set with the full-coverage set. Indeed the intersections are a directed basis by the same minimum construction, and every common refinement contains them. This gives the basis $B_c(r)$ at level $c$.

The full filter is the supremum over $c$ of those fixed-level filters. In the filter order a finer filter is smaller, and the sets of a supremum are the intersection of the collections of filter members. Thus $A$ belongs to the full filter exactly when for every $c$ there exists an allowed $r_c$ with $B_c(r_c)\subseteq A$. Choose these witnesses as a family. The simultaneous inclusions are equivalent to the displayed union inclusion. This is the substance of the pinned use of the principal-filter basis, `inf_principal` and `hasBasis_iSup`. In the full-coverage specialization, the top untagged partition has union $K$, so equality of unions becomes $\mathrm{IsPartition}$. [Pinned basis proofs][ch3-n6a-basis]

**Theorem (epsilon–gauge characterization).** [Lean, read]. With the data above and a prescribed $y\in V$, $\mathrm{HasIntegral}(K,\ell,f,\mathrm{vol},y)$ holds if and only if
$$
\forall\varepsilon>0,\quad \exists r:[0,\infty)\to(\mathbb R^\iota\to(0,\infty)),\quad
(\forall c,\ R_\ell(r_c))\ \land\
(\forall c\,\forall\pi,\ \pi\in B_c(r_c)\Rightarrow d(S(\pi),y)\le\varepsilon).
$$
Here $\pi$ ranges over tagged prepartitions, and membership in $B_c$ includes full coverage.

*Proof.* Suppose the sums tend to $y$ along $\mathcal F_\ell(K,\top)$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, the closed ball of radius $\varepsilon$ about $y$ is a neighborhood, so its inverse image under $S$ belongs to that filter. Apply the lemma with this inverse image as $A$. If $\pi\in B_c(r_c)$ for any given $c$, it belongs to the union and hence its sum lies in the closed ball.

Conversely, fix any neighborhood $U$ of $y$. An open ball of some radius $\eta>0$ lies inside $U$, so the closed ball of radius $\eta/2$ lies inside $U$. Apply the displayed condition at $\eta/2$. Any partition in the resulting union has a level $c$ witnessing its membership, so the estimate at that level places its sum in $U$. The union is contained in $S^{-1}(U)$, which therefore belongs to the filter. This is convergence to $y$. The Lean body applies the basis criterion for $\mathrm{Tendsto}$ to the full-partition basis and the positive-radius closed-ball basis. Its final simplification turns an implication from an existential level witness into an estimate for every level and reorders the two universal quantifiers. [Pinned equivalence proof][ch3-n6a-basic]

**Hand computation (one dimension).** Take $K=(0,1]$, $\ell=\mathrm{GP}$, ordinary length, and $f(t)=t$. A full partition has at least one cell, every interval has distortion $1$, and the complement is empty. Thus $B_c(r)$ is empty for $c<1$, and for $c\ge1$ it equals $B_1(r)$. If $[\alpha_j,\beta_j]$ are the closed cells, $h_j=\beta_j-\alpha_j$ and $t_j$ are the tags, then
$$
S(\pi)-\tfrac12=\sum_j h_j\bigl(t_j-(\alpha_j+\beta_j)/2\bigr).
$$
Indeed the integrals of $t$ on those cells are $h_j(\alpha_j+\beta_j)/2$ and sum to $1/2$. For the constant gauge $\varepsilon$, the midpoint of every cell is within $\varepsilon$ of its tag, so the absolute error is at most $\varepsilon\sum_j h_j=\varepsilon$. One can use the same gauge at every level. Conversely any bound certified at level $1$ extends to all levels with its level-$1$ witness repeated. For the equal intervals with tags $j/N$ and $(j-1)/N$, the distances from the far endpoints are $1/N$ in each case. Both partitions lie in $B_1(1/N)$, with sums $(N+1)/(2N)$ and $(N-1)/(2N)$ respectively, by the arithmetic progression formula. In particular the level-$1$ condition determines the limit, whereas level $0$ alone admits every proposed value. At any positive constant radius the two families occur once $N$ is large enough. For arbitrary positive gauges, the existence of sufficiently fine level-$1$ partitions is supplied by the [Cousin corollary](#cousins-lemma-fine-tagged-partitions-of-a-box).

**Hand computation (two dimensions).** Let $K=(0,1]^2$, again with GP parameters, and let $f(x,y)=y$ with area as volume. For positive integers $m,N$, divide $K$ into $mN$ columns and $N$ rows, tagging each cell at the midpoint of its top edge. Each of the $mN^2$ cells has area $1/(mN^2)$, distortion $m$, and maximum sup-norm distance $1/N$ from its tag. Row $j$ has area $1/N$ and tag height $j/N$, so
$$S(\pi_{m,N})=\sum_{j=1}^N j/N^2=(N+1)/(2N).$$
The integral value is $1/2$: on any partition fine for the constant gauge $\varepsilon$, the cell-average height differs from the tag height by at most $\varepsilon$, and the total area is $1$. The exact error on the displayed partitions is $1/(2N)$, independently of $m$.

The geometry makes two distinctions visible even though this particular integrand has a uniform error estimate.

1. Let $A_1$ consist of full partitions into squares. Every $B_1(r)$ is contained in $A_1$. For **any** positive gauge at level $2$, divide $K$ into the two half-width rectangles and use Cousin's lemma to bisect each rectangle into fine tagged cells while preserving its ratio $2$. This is the fixed-shape construction in the Cousin subsection. The resulting full partition lies in $B_2(r)$ and outside $A_1$. For constant radius $\rho$ an explicit instance is $\pi_{2,N}$ with $N\ge1/\rho$. Thus $A_1$ is a level-$1$ filter member and fails to belong to the all-level filter.
2. For a cell $J$, write $h(J)$ for its longest side and $\kappa(J)$ for its distortion. Let $A$ be the full Henstock partitions satisfying $h(J)\kappa(J)\le1$ for all cells. Use the family $r_c\equiv1/(2\max(1,c))$. A fine cell has longest side at most $2r_c$, and $\kappa(J)\le c$, giving $h(J)\kappa(J)\le1$. Thus $A$ contains the all-level basis union. Now let $r$ be any single positive gauge. Choose $p=(1/2,1/2)$ and $0<h<\min(r(p),1/2)$. Insert the cell $(1/2,1/2+w]\times(1/2,1/2+h]$ tagged at $p$, with $0<w<h^2$. This cell is fine, but $h\kappa=h^2/w>1$. For example $w=h^2/2$ gives exactly $2$. Cut at its faces and take fine Henstock partitions of the other rectangles by Cousin's lemma. The resulting full partition is fine for $r$ and has some finite distortion level, yet lies outside $A$. With constant radius $\rho$, the explicit grid $\pi_{m,N}$ with $N\ge1/\rho$ and $m>N$ gives the same obstruction, since each $h(J)\kappa(J)=m/N>1$. Consequently no basis union with one repeated gauge is contained in $A$. The separation concerns the filter bases themselves.

**Remarks.**

1. *Which direction supplies Henstock–Sacks?* Given an integrability witness, $\mathrm{Integrable.hasIntegral}$ supplies the limit at the chosen integral. For $\varepsilon>0$, $\mathrm{convergenceR}$ chooses the family from `hasIntegral_iff.1`, and `convergenceR_cond` and `dist_integralSum_integral_le_of_memBaseSet` project its two properties. For $\varepsilon\le0$ the definition uses radius $1$ and its gauge condition, while the estimate requires positive $\varepsilon$. For equal-union prepartitions the first Henstock–Sacks lemma completes both using the pointwise minimum of the two chosen gauges and applies the full-partition estimate twice. In the later finite-cell step, each subbox $J$ receives its own convergence gauge at the chosen common level $C$, paired with the ambient gauge. Subbox integrability itself comes from the filter-limit composition in N6d. The reverse implication of the theorem above certifies a prescribed limit from gauge estimates and supplies the comparison proof described below. [Gauge consumers][ch3-n6a-consume] [Subbox consumer][ch3-n6a-subbox] [Finite-cell step][ch3-n6a-hs]

   *The reverse direction in the box comparison.* `hasIntegralIndicatorConst` uses `HasIntegral.of_mul` to convert the indicator estimate for a measurable set and locally finite measure into a box limit. `HasIntegral.of_aeEq_zero` uses `hasIntegral_iff.2` directly for a function vanishing almost everywhere for the restricted measure. Both calls allow gauges to vary with the tag (`bRiemann = false`). `IntegrableOn.hasBoxIntegral` uses `HasIntegral.of_mul` again for its simple-function approximation estimate under the same gauge and measure conditions and with a complete target. The grouped error in that estimate uses Henstock–Sacks. Rectangular Bochner divergence then invokes `IntegrableOn.hasBoxIntegral` at GP and identifies its limit with the GP divergence limit by uniqueness. [Indicator](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Integrability.lean#L39-L45) · [Null function](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Integrability.lean#L102-L110) · [Comparison and grouped error](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Integrability.lean#L198-L285) · [Rescaling estimate](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/Analysis/BoxIntegral/Basic.lean#L207-L216) · [Divergence consumer](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/0df444a360eaa60ab8c11dca51a86af692955474/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/DivergenceTheorem.lean#L118-L136)

2. *Nontriviality and completeness.* The equivalence holds even for a bottom filter. On $(0,1]$, the fixed GP level $0$ illustrates what goes wrong with uniqueness if nontriviality is removed: with $f\equiv0$ both $0$ and $1$ meet the empty-basis estimates for all $\varepsilon>0$. The actual full filter is nontrivial by the Cousin corollary, which also provides the ingredient used in uniqueness. Completeness is unnecessary when the candidate $y$ is given. It enters when converting Cauchy control into existence of $y$. The $c_{00}$ example in the [subbox subsection](#subbox-filters-and-the-cauchy-net-of-tagged-sums) gives Cauchy sums on $(0,1]$ approaching $(2^{-k})_{k\ge1}$ in the sup norm of the completion. Every candidate in $c_{00}$ vanishes in some coordinate $k$ and is therefore excluded by sufficiently fine sums at tolerance $2^{-k-1}$. Conversely the constant $e_1$ has integral $e_1$ there, since the lengths add to $1$; the box integral can be nonzero in an incomplete space.
3. *Sources and reading scope.* Fremlin defines the gauge filter from fine full partitions and defines the integral by convergence along it [Fremlin2003Ch48, §§481C–F] [Paper]. His vector-valued note makes the completeness step explicit in §1D(b) [Fremlin2010] [Paper]. The separate GP levels and their supremum here come from Yury Kudryashov's pinned Mathlib construction. The basis proof, equivalence and direct consumer calls are read against the pinned source. General filter and metric API proof bodies and the oscillation argument of `integrable_of_bounded_and_ae_continuousWithinAt` remain named inputs. [Pinned basis proofs][ch3-n6a-basis]
